[{"q":"How do you design immutable classes and what are their benefits?","g":[],"a":"<p>Immutable classes are designed so their state cannot be changed after creation. Key design principles:</p>\n<p><strong>Design Guidelines:</strong></p>\n<ol>\n<li>Make all fields private and final</li>\n<li>Don&#39;t provide setter methods</li>\n<li>Don&#39;t allow subclassing (make class final)</li>\n<li>If fields are mutable objects, return defensive copies</li>\n<li>Initialize all fields via constructor</li>\n</ol>\n<p><strong>JavaScript Example:</strong></p>\n<div differ in how they handle nested objects and references.</p>\n<p><strong>Shallow Copy:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li>Copies object&#39;s immediate properties</li>\n<li>Nested objects share references with original</li>\n<li>Changes to nested objects affect both copies</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>Deep Copy:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li>Recursively copies all nested objects</li>\n<li>Creates completely independent copy</li>\n<li>Changes to nested objects don&#39;t affect original</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>JavaScript Example:</strong></p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div    };\n    \n    <i class=k>return</i> cloned;\n  }\n  \n  <i class=c>// Generic deep clone using JSON (limited)</i>\n  <i class=f>jsonClone</i>(<i class=p></i>) {\n    <i class=k>const</i> json = <i <i class=k>import</i> datetime\n<i class=k>from</i> typing <i class=k>import</i> <i class=t>List</i>, <i class=t>Any</i>\n\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>Cloneable</i>(<i class=f>ABC</i>):\n<i class=m>    @abstractmethod</i>\n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>__copy__</i>(<i class=p>self</i>):\n        <i class=k>pass</i>\n    \n<i class=m>    @abstractmethod  </i>\n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>__deepcopy__</i>(<i class=p>self, memo</i>):\n        <i class=k>pass</i>\n\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>Book</i>(<i class=f>Cloneable</i>):\n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=s>&quot;created&quot;</i>: datetime.now(), <i class=s>&quot;views&quot;</i>: <i class=n>0</i>}\n    \n    <i class=c># Shallow copy implementation</i>\n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>__copy__</i>(<i class=p>self</i>):\n        <i class=c># Create new instance without calling __init__</i>\n        cls = <i class=v>self</i>.__class__\n        result = cls.__new__(cls)\n        <i class=c># Copy instance dictionary</i>\n        result.__dict__.update(<i class=v>self</i>.__dict__)\n        <i class=k>return</i> result\n    \n    <i class=c># Deep copy implementation  </i>\n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>__deepcopy__</i>(<i class=p>self, memo</i>):\n        cls = <i class=v>self</i>.__class__\n        result = cls.__new__(cls)\n        memo[<i class=b>id</i>(<i class=v>self</i>)] = result  <i class=c># Prevent infinite recursion</i>\n        \n        <i class=k>for</i> key, value <i class=k>in</i> <i class=v>self</i>.__dict__.items():\n            <i class=b>setattr</i>(result, key, copy.deepcopy(value, memo))\n        <i class=k>return</i> result\n    \n    <i class=c># Alternative manual deep copy</i>\n    <i class=k>def</i> <i memo[<i class=b>id</i>(<i class=v>self</i>)]\n            \n        cloned = Document(<i class=v>self</i>.content)\n        memo[<i class=b>id</i>(<i class=v>self</i>)] = cloned\n        cloned._cache occurs when changes to a base class break derived classes in unexpected ways, even when the changes seem safe.</p>\n<p><strong>Common Problems:</strong></p>\n<p><strong>1. Method Addition Conflicts:</strong></p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div class=\"code-head\"><span>javascript</span><button class=\"copy\" type=\"button\">Copy</button></div><pre><code class=\"hljs\"><i class=c>// Base class (version 1)</i>\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>DataProcessor</i> {\n  <i class=f>process</i>(<i class=p>data</i>) {\n    <i class=k>return</i> <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=f>transform</i>(data);\n  }\n}\n\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>UserProcessor</i> <i class=k>extends</i> <i class=f>DataProcessor</i> {\n  <i class=c>// Custom method</i>\n  <i class=f>normalize</i>(<i class=p>data</i>) {\n    <i class=k>return</i> data.<i class=f>toLowerCase</i>();\n  }\n}\n\n<i class=c>// Base class (version 2) - adds normalize method</i>\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>DataProcessor</i> {\n  <i class=f>process</i>(<i class=p>data</i>) {\n    <i class=k>return</i> <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=f>normalize</i>(<i class=v>this</i>.<i class=f>transform</i>(data));\n  }\n  \n  <i class=c>// New method conflicts with derived class</i>\n  <i class=f>normalize</i>(<i class=p>data</i>) {\n    <i class=k>return</i> data.<i class=f>trim</i>();\n  }\n}\n<i class=c>// Now UserProcessor.normalize behavior changes unexpectedly!</i></code></pre></div>\n<p><strong>2. Self-Use Problems:</strong></p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div class=\"code-head\"><span>javascript</span><button class=\"copy\" type=\"button\">Copy</button></div><pre><code class=\"hljs\"><i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>Counter</i> {\n  <i class=f>constructor</i>(<i class=p></i>) {\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>count</i> = <i class=n>0</i>;\n  }\n  \n  <i class=f>increment</i>(<i class=p></i>) {\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>count</i>++;\n  }\n  \n  <i class=f>incrementBy</i>(<i class=p>n</i>) {\n    <i class=k>for</i> (<i class=k>let</i> i = <i class=n>0</i>; i &lt; n; i++) {\n      <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=f>increment</i>(); <i class=c>// Self-use</i>\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>TrackedCounter</i> <i class=k>extends</i> <i class=f>Counter</i> {\n  <i class=f>constructor</i>(<i class=p></i>) {\n    <i class=v>super</i>();\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>operations</i> = [];\n  }\n  \n  <i class=f>increment</i>(<i class=p></i>) {\n    <i class=v>super</i>.<i class=f>increment</i>();\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>operations</i>.<i class=f>push</i>(<i class=s>&#x27;increment&#x27;</i>);\n  }\n}\n\n<i class=k>const</i> counter = <i class=k>new</i> <i class=f>TrackedCounter</i>();\ncounter.<i class=f>incrementBy</i>(<i class=n>3</i>); Favor Composition Over Inheritance</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li>Use delegation instead of inheritance</li>\n<li>More flexible and less fragile</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>2. Design for Extension</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li>Use abstract base classes with clear contracts</li>\n<li>Provide hook methods for customization</li>\n<li>Document extension points</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>3. Example: Teacher-Student relationship - teachers can exist without students and vice versa.</p>\n<p><strong>Aggregation</strong> is a &#39;has-a&#39; relationship where the container doesn&#39;t own the contained objects. Example: Department-Employee - department has employees, but employees can exist without the department.</p>\n<p><strong>Composition</strong> is a strong &#39;owns-a&#39; relationship where the container controls the lifecycle of contained objects. Example: House-Room - rooms cannot exist without the house.</p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div class=\"code-head\"><span>javascript</span><button class=\"copy\" type=\"button\">Copy</button></div><pre><code class=\"hljs\"><i class=c>// Association</i>\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>Teacher</i> {\n  <i class=f>constructor</i>(<i class=p>name</i>) {\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>name</i> = name;\n  }\n  \n  <i class=f>teach</i>(<i class=p>student</i>) {\n    <i class=v>console</i>.<i class=f>log</i>(<i class=s>`<i class=u>${<i class=v>this</i>.name}</i> teaches <i class=u>${student.name}</i>`</i>);\n  }\n}\n\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>Student</i> {\n  <i class=f>constructor</i>(<i class=p>name</i>) {\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>name</i> = name;\n  }\n}\n\n<i class=c>// Aggregation</i>\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>Department</i> {\n  <i class=f>constructor</i>(<i class=p>name</i>) {\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>name</i> = name;\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>employees</i> = [];\n  }\n  \n  <i class=f>addEmployee</i>(<i class=p>employee</i>) {\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i composition?","g":[],"a":"<p><strong>Deep Inheritance Hierarchies:</strong></p>\n<p><em>Advantages:</em></p>\n<ul>\n<li>Code reuse through inheritance</li>\n<li>Clear taxonomical relationships</li>\n<li>Polymorphic behavior</li>\n<li>Easy to understand &#39;is-a&#39; relationships</li>\n</ul>\n<p><em>Disadvantages:</em></p>\n<ul>\n<li>Tight coupling between classes</li>\n<li>Fragile base class problem</li>\n<li>Diamond problem in multiple inheritance</li>\n<li>Hard to modify without affecting subclasses</li>\n<li>Inflexible at runtime</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>Flat Composition:</strong></p>\n<p><em>Advantages:</em></p>\n<ul>\n<li>Loose coupling</li>\n<li>Runtime flexibility</li>\n<li>Easy to test (mock dependencies)</li>\n<li>Follows &#39;has-a&#39; relationships</li>\n<li>Better maintainability</li>\n</ul>\n<p><em>Disadvantages:</em></p>\n<ul>\n<li>More boilerplate code</li>\n<li>Can be complex to understand</li>\n<li>Potential for code duplication</li>\n<li>Less intuitive for natural hierarchies</li>\n</ul>\n<div class=\"code\"><div class=\"code-head\"><span>javascript</span><button class=\"copy\" type=\"button\">Copy</button></div><pre><code class=\"hljs\"><i class=c>// Deep Inheritance (Problematic)</i>\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>Animal</i> {\n  <i Liskov Substitution Principle (LSP) states that objects of a superclass should be replaceable with objects of a subclass without breaking the application. Subclasses should strengthen preconditions and weaken postconditions.</p>\n<p><strong>LSP Violation Example:</strong></p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div class=\"code-head\"><span>javascript</span><button dependencies between classes?","g":[],"a":"<p>Circular dependencies occur when two or more classes depend on each other, creating a cycle. Here are several strategies to resolve them:</p>\n<p><strong>1. Dependency Injection</strong><br><strong>2. Observer Pattern</strong><br><strong>3. Mediator Pattern</strong><br><strong>4. Interface Segregation</strong><br><strong>5. Lazy Loading/Forward Declarations</strong></p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div class=\"code-head\"><span>javascript</span><button class=\"copy\" type=\"button\">Copy</button></div><pre><code class=\"hljs\"><i class=c>// Problem: Circular Dependency</i>\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>User</i> {\n  <i class=f>constructor</i>(<i class=p>name</i>) {\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>name</i> = name;\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>orders</i> = [];\n  }\n  \n  <i class=f>addOrder</i>(<i class=p>order</i>) {\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>orders</i>.<i class=f>constructor</i>(<i class=p>id</i>) {\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>id</i> = id;\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>user</i> = <i class=l>null</i>;\n  }\n  \n  <i class=f>setUser</i>(<i class=f>addOrder</i>(<i class=v>this</i>); <i class=c>// Circular call</i>\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n<i class=c>// Solution 1: Dependency Injection</i>\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>UserService</i> {\n  <i class=f>linkUserOrder</i>(userId, orderId);\n    <i class=k>return</i> order;\n  }\n  \n  <i class=f>linkUserOrder</i>(<i class=p>userId, orderId</i>) {\n    <i class=k>const</i> user = <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>users</i>.<i class=f>get</i>(userId);\n    <i class=k>const</i> order = <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>orders</i>.<i class=f>get</i>(orderId);\n    <i class=k>if</i> (user &amp;&amp; order) {\n      user.<i class=v>orderIds</i>.<i class=f>push</i>(orderId);\n      order.<i class=v>userId</i> = userId;\n    }\n  }\n  \n  <i class=f>getUserOrders</i>(<i class=p>userId</i>) {\n    <i class=k>const</i> user = <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>users</i>.<i class=f>get</i>(userId);\n    <i class=k>return</i> user ? user.<i class=v>orderIds</i>.<i class=f>map</i>(<i class=function><i class=p>id</i> =&gt;</i> <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>orders</i>.<i class=f>get</i>(id)) : [];\n  }\n}\n\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>IsolatedUser</i> {\n  <i class=f>constructor</i>(<i class=p>name</i>) {\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>id</i> = <i class=f>Math</i>.<i class=f>random</i>().<i class=f>toString</i>(<i class=n>36</i>);\n    <i  <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>events</i>[event].<i class=f>forEach</i>(<i class=function><i class=p>callback</i> =&gt;</i> <i class=f>callback</i>(data));\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n<i class=k>const</i> class=s>&#x27;userOrderCreated&#x27;</i>, <i class=function>(<i class=p>data</i>) =&gt;</i> {\n      <i class=k>if</i> (data.<i class=v>orderId</i> === <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>id</i>) {\n        eventBus.<i class=f>emit</i>(<i class=s>&#x27;orderCreated&#x27;</i>, <i class=v>this</i>);\n      }\n    });\n  }\n}</code></pre></div>\n<div class=\"code\"><div       <i class=v>self</i>.user = <i class=l>None</i>\n    \n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>set_user</i>(<i class=p>self, user</i>):\n        <i class=v>self</i>.user = user\n        <i performance with direct function calls and no runtime lookup overhead. Dynamic binding resolves calls at runtime through vtables or method lookup tables, adding 1-2 CPU cycles overhead but enabling polymorphism. Static binding allows aggressive compiler optimizations like inlining, while dynamic binding requires indirection through function pointers, making it ~10-20% slower but essential for OOP flexibility.</p>\n<p><strong>JavaScript Example:</strong></p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div class=\"code-head\"><span>javascript</span><button class=\"copy\" type=\"button\">Copy</button></div><pre><code class=\"hljs\"><i class=c>// Static-like binding (direct property access)</i>\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>Rectangle</i> {\n  <i class=f>area</i>(<i class=p></i>) { <i class=k>return</i> <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>width</i> * <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>height</i>; advantages/disadvantages.","g":[],"a":"<p>Duck typing follows the principle &#39;If it walks like a duck and quacks like a duck, then it&#39;s a duck&#39; - focusing on object behavior rather than explicit type declarations. This enables flexible polymorphism and rapid prototyping but sacrifices compile-time type safety and can lead to runtime errors.</p>\n<p><strong>Advantages:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li><strong>Flexibility</strong>: Objects don&#39;t need common inheritance hierarchy</li>\n<li><strong>Rapid Development</strong>: Less boilerplate type declarations</li>\n<li><strong>Protocol-based Design</strong>: Focus on interfaces rather than implementations</li>\n<li><strong>Easy Testing</strong>: Mock objects just need required methods</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>Disadvantages:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li><strong>Runtime Errors</strong>: Type mismatches discovered at runtime</li>\n<li><strong>Documentation</strong>: Implicit contracts harder to understand</li>\n<li><strong>IDE Support</strong>: Limited autocomplete and refactoring</li>\n<li><strong>Performance</strong>: Dynamic method lookup overhead</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>JavaScript - Duck Typing Examples:</strong></p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div class=\"code-head\"><span>javascript</span><button class=\"copy\" It establishes a parent-child relationship through class extension.</p>\n<p><strong>JavaScript Example:</strong></p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div class=\"code-head\"><span>javascript</span><button class=\"copy\" type=\"button\">Copy</button></div><pre><code class=\"hljs\"><i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>Animal</i> {\n  <i class=f>constructor</i>(<i class=p>name</i>) {\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>name</i> = name;\n  }\n  <i class=f>speak</i>(<i class=p></i>) {\n    <i class=v>console</i>.<i class=f>log</i>(<i class=s>`<i class=u>${<i class=v>this</i>.name}</i> makes a sound`</i>);\n  }\n}\n\n<i class=c>// Dog IS-A Animal</i>\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>Dog</i> <i class=k>extends</i> <i class=f>Animal</i> {\n  <i class=f>speak</i>(<i class=p></i>) {\n    <i class=v>console</i>.<i class=f>log</i>(<i class=s>`<i class=u>${<i class=v>this</i>.name}</i> barks`</i>);\n  }\n}\n\n<i class=k>const</i> dog = <i class=k>new</i> <i class=f>Dog</i>(<i class=s>&quot;Buddy&quot;</i>);\ndog.<i class=f>speak</i>(); The contained object is part of the containing object.</p>\n<p><strong>JavaScript Example:</strong></p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div class=\"code-head\"><span>javascript</span><button class=\"copy\" (Dependency):</strong><br>Represents a temporary relationship where one class uses another class&#39;s methods or services, typically through method parameters or local variables.</p>\n<p><strong>JavaScript Example:</strong></p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div class=\"code-head\"><span>javascript</span><button class=\"copy\" type=\"button\">Copy</button></div><pre><code class=\"hljs\"><i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>Logger</i> {\n  <i class=f>log</i>(<i class=p>message</i>) {\n    <i class=v>console</i>.<i class=f>log</i>(<i class=s>`[LOG]: <i Differences:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li><strong>IS-A</strong>: Permanent relationship, shared interface, polymorphism</li>\n<li><strong>HAS-A</strong>: Ownership relationship, encapsulation, lifecycle management</li>\n<li><strong>USES-A</strong>: Temporary relationship, loose coupling, dependency injection</li>\n</ul>"},{"q":"How do you handle versioning in object class=s>&quot;1.0&quot;</i>;\n    \n    <i class=c>// Validate current schema</i>\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=f>validateSchema</i>(data, currentVersion);\n    \n    <i class=c>// Migrate if class=k>const</i> schema = <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>schemas</i>[version];\n    <i class=k>if</i> (!schema) <i class=k>throw</i> <i class=k>new</i> <i class=f>Error</i>(<i class=s>`Unknown schema class=\"hljs\"><i class=k>from</i> dataclasses <i class=k>import</i> dataclass, field\n<i class=k>from</i> typing <i class=k>import</i> <i class=t>Dict</i>, <i class=t>Any</i>, <i class=s>&quot;email&quot;</i>, <i class=s>&quot;version&quot;</i>]})\n            <i class=k>return</i> UserV2.from_v1(v1_user)\n        <i class=k>elif</i> version == <i class=s>&quot;2.0&quot;</i>:\n            <i class=k>return</i> UserV2(**data)\n        <i class=k>else</i>:\n            <i class=k>raise</i> ValueError(<i class=s>f&quot;Unsupported version: <i class=u>{version}</i>&quot;</i>)\n    \n<i class=m>    @classmethod</i>\n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>serialize</i>(<i class=p>cls, user: UserV2</i>) -&gt; <i class=b>str</i>:\n        <i class=k>return</i> json.dumps({\n            <i class=s>&quot;name&quot;</i>: user.name,\n            <i class=s>&quot;email&quot;</i>: user.email,\n            <i class=s>&quot;preferences&quot;</i>: user.preferences,\n            <i class=s>&quot;last_login&quot;</i>: user.last_login.isoformat() <i class=k>if</i> user.last_login <i class=k>else</i> <i class=l>None</i>,\n            <i class=s>&quot;version&quot;</i>: user.version\n        })\n\n<i class=c># Example usage</i>\nold_data = <i class=s>&#x27;{&quot;name&quot;: &quot;John&quot;, &quot;email&quot;: &quot;john@email.com&quot;, &quot;version&quot;: &quot;1.0&quot;}&#x27;</i>\nuser = UserSerializer.deserialize(old_data)\n<i class=b>print</i>(<i class=s>f&quot;Migrated user: <i class=u>{user}</i>&quot;</i>)</code></pre></div>\n<p><strong>Key Versioning Strategies:</strong></p>\n<ol>\n<li><strong>Version Fields</strong>: Explicit version numbers in serialized data</li>\n<li><strong>Schema Migration</strong>: Transform old formats to new formats</li>\n<li><strong>Backward Compatibility</strong>: Support reading old versions</li>\n<li><strong>Forward Compatibility</strong>: Gracefully handle unknown fields</li>\n<li><strong>Default Values</strong>: Provide sensible defaults for new fields</li>\n<li><strong>Validation</strong>: Ensure data but exist only at compile-time for type safety. They help enforce constraints and provide additional type information without runtime overhead.</p>\n<p><strong>JavaScript Example (using TypeScript for type safety):</strong></p>\n<div amount;\n    <i class=v>this</i>[<i class=v>CURRENCY_TYPE</i>] = currencyType;\n  }\n  \n  <i class=k>static</i> <i class=f>usd</i>(<i class=p>amount</i>) {\n    <i class=k>return</i> <i class=f>meters</i>(<i class=p>value</i>) {\n    <i class=k>return</i> <i class=k>new</i> <i class=f>Measurement</i>(value, <i class=s>&#x27;METERS&#x27;</i>);\n  }\n  \n  <i class=k>static</i> <i   <i class=k>return</i> cls(Locked)\n    \n<i class=m>    @classmethod</i>\n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>unlocked</i>(<i class=p>cls</i>) -&gt; <i class=s>&#x27;Door[Unlocked]&#x27;</i>:\n        <i class=k>return</i> cls(Unlocked)\n    \n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>unlock</i>(<i class=p>self: <i class=s>&#x27;Door[Locked]&#x27;</i></i>) -&gt; <i class=s>&#x27;Door[Unlocked]&#x27;</i>:\n class=u>{self._state_type.__name__.lower()}</i>&quot;</i>\n\n<i class=c># State machine usage</i>\nlocked_door = Door.locked()\nunlocked_door = locked_door.unlock()\nunlocked_door.<i class=b>open</i>()  <i class=c># Valid</i>\n<i class=c># locked_door.open()  # Would raise TypeError</i>\n\n<i class=c># Example with database connection states</i>\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=b>str</i>:\n        <i class=k>if</i> <i class=v>self</i>._state_type != Connected:\n            <i class=k>raise</i> TypeError(<i class=s>&quot;Must be connected to execute queries&quot;</i>)\n        <i class=k>return</i> <i class=s>f&quot;Executing: <i class=u>{sql}</i>&quot;</i>\n    \n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>disconnect</i>(<i class=p>self: <i class=n>10</i>)\ndistance2 = Quantity.meters(<i class=n>5</i>)\ntotal_distance = distance1 + distance2\n<i class=b>print</i>(total_distance)  <i class=c># 15 m</i>\n\ntime = Quantity.seconds(<i Types:</strong></p>\n<ol>\n<li><strong>Compile-time Safety</strong>: Catch type errors early</li>\n<li><strong>Zero Runtime Cost</strong>: No performance overhead</li>\n<li><strong>API Design</strong>: Create more expressive and safe APIs</li>\n<li><strong>State Machine Modeling</strong>: Enforce valid state transitions</li>\n<li><strong>Unit Safety</strong>: Prevent mixing incompatible units</li>\n<li><strong>Resource Management</strong>: Ensure proper resource lifecycle</li>\n</ol>"},{"q":"What are the four main principles of Object-Oriented Programming?","g":["OOP principles"],"a":"<ol>\n<li>Encapsulation (bundling data and methods), 2. possible</li>\n<li><strong>Synchronization</strong>: Use locks/mutexes for mutable state</li>\n<li><strong>Atomic Operations</strong>: Use atomic variables for simple operations</li>\n<li><strong>Thread-local Storage</strong>: Keep state per thread</li>\n<li><strong>Lock-free Programming</strong>: Use concurrent data structures</li>\n</ol>\n<p><strong>JavaScript nested locks to prevent deadlocks</li>\n<li>Consider using concurrent collections</li>\n<li>Document thread-safety guarantees</li>\n</ul>"},{"q":"What is the difference between intrinsic and extrinsic state in the Flyweight pattern?","g":[],"a":"<p>The Flyweight pattern separates object state into two categories to minimize memory usage:</p>\n<p><strong>Intrinsic State:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li>Shared among multiple objects</li>\n<li>Stored inside the flyweight object</li>\n<li>Independent of context</li>\n<li>Immutable</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>Extrinsic State:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li>Unique to each object instance</li>\n<li>Passed as parameters to flyweight methods</li>\n<li>Context-dependent</li>\n<li>Can be mutable</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>JavaScript Example:</strong></p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div class=\"code-head\"><span>javascript</span><button class=\"copy\" = font;           <i class=c>// intrinsic</i>\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>size</i> = size;           <i class=c>// intrinsic</i>\n  }\n\n  <i class=c>// Extrinsic state passed as parameters</i>\n  <i class=f>render</i>(<i class=p>x, y, color, context</i>) {\n    context.<i class=v>fillStyle</i> = color;  <i class=c>// extrinsic</i>\n    context.<i class=v>font</i> = <i class=c>// x, y are extrinsic</i>\n  }\n}\n\n<i class=c>// Flyweight Factory</i>\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>CharacterFactory</i> {\n  <i class=f>constructor</i>(<i class=p></i>) {\n    <i class=v>flyweights</i>.<i class=v>size</i>;\n  }\n}\n\n<i class=c>// Context: Document (manages extrinsic state)</i>\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>Document</i> {\n  <i class=f>constructor</i>(<i usage</li>\n<li><strong>Performance</strong>: Fewer object creations</li>\n<li><strong>Scalability</strong>: Can handle large numbers of similar objects</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>When to Use:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li>Large numbers of similar objects</li>\n<li>Object creation is expensive</li>\n<li>Most object state can be made extrinsic</li>\n</ul>"},{"q":"What is the difference between composition and delegation?","g":[],"a":"<p>Composition is a structural relationship where one object contains another object as a part of its implementation. The containing object owns the contained object and their lifetimes are typically tied together. Delegation is a behavioral pattern where one object forwards requests to another object to handle the work, but doesn&#39;t necessarily own the delegate object.</p>\n<p>Key Differences:</p>\n<ul>\n<li>Ownership: Composition implies ownership; delegation doesn&#39;t</li>\n<li>Lifetime: In composition, contained objects are created/destroyed with the container; in delegation, delegate objects can exist independently</li>\n<li>Relationship: Composition is has-a; delegation is uses-a</li>\n</ul>\n<p>JavaScript Examples:</p>\n<p>javascript<br>// Composition Example<br>class Engine {<br>start() {<br>console.log(&#39;Engine starting...&#39;);<br>}<br>}</p>\n<p>class Car {<br>constructor() {<br>this.engine = new Engine(); // Car owns the engine<br>}</p>\n<p>start() {<br>this.engine.start();<br>}<br>}</p>\n<p>// Delegation Example<br>class Logger {<br>log(message) {<br>console.log(<code>[LOG]: ${message}</code>);<br>}<br>}</p>\n<p>class UserService {<br>constructor(logger) {<br>this.logger = logger; Specifically, a method M of object O should only invoke methods of:</p>\n<ol>\n<li>O itself</li>\n<li>M&#39;s parameters</li>\n<li>Objects created within M</li>\n<li>O&#39;s direct component objects</li>\n</ol>\n<p>Benefits:</p>\n<ul>\n<li>Reduces coupling between classes</li>\n<li>Makes code more maintainable</li>\n<li>Prevents chain reactions when changes occur</li>\n<li>Improves encapsulation</li>\n</ul>\n<p>JavaScript Violation Example:</p>\n<p>javascript<br>// VIOLATION - Chain of method calls<br>class Customer {<br>constructor(wallet) {<br>this.wallet = wallet;<br>}<br>}</p>\n<p>class Wallet {<br>constructor(money) {<br>this.money = money;<br>}</p>\n<p>getMoney() {<br>return this.money;<br>}<br>}</p>\n<p>class Money {<br>constructor(amount) {<br>this.amount = amount;<br>}</p>\n<p>getAmount() {<br>return this.amount;<br>}<br>}</p>\n<p>// BAD: Violates Law of Demeter<br>class ShoppingCart {<br>calculateTotal(customer) {<br>// Too much knowledge about customer&#39;s internal structure<br>const amount = customer.wallet.getMoney().getAmount(); // VIOLATION!<br>return amount * 0.1;<br>}<br>}</p>\n<p>// GOOD: Following Law of Demeter<br>class Customer {<br>constructor(wallet) {<br>this.wallet = wallet;<br>}</p>\n<p>// Provide a direct method<br>getAvailableAmount() {<br>return this.wallet.getMoney().getAmount();<br>}<br>}</p>\n<p>class ShoppingCart {<br>calculateTotal(customer) {<br>const amount = customer.getAvailableAmount(); // GOOD!<br>return amount * 0.1;<br>}<br>}</p>\n<p>Python Violation Example:</p>\n<p>python</p>\n<h1>VIOLATION - Chain of method calls</h1>\n<p>class Customer:<br>def <strong>init</strong>(self, wallet):<br>self.wallet = wallet</p>\n<p>class Wallet:<br>def <strong>init</strong>(self, money):<br>self.money = money</p>\n<p>def get_money(self):<br>return self.money</p>\n<p>class Money:<br>def <strong>init</strong>(self, amount):<br>self.amount = amount</p>\n<p>def get_amount(self):<br>return self.amount</p>\n<h1>BAD: Violates Law of Demeter</h1>\n<p>class ShoppingCart:<br>def calculate_total(self, customer):</p>\n<h1>Too much knowledge about customer&#39;s internal structure</h1>\n<p>amount = customer.wallet.get_money().get_amount() # VIOLATION!<br>return amount * 0.1</p>\n<h1>GOOD: Following Law of Demeter</h1>\n<p>class Customer:<br>def <strong>init</strong>(self, wallet):<br>self.wallet = wallet</p>\n<h1>Provide a direct method</h1>\n<p>def get_available_amount(self):<br>return self.wallet.get_money().get_amount()</p>\n<p>class ShoppingCart:<br>def calculate_total(self, customer):<br>amount = customer.get_available_amount() # GOOD!<br>return amount * 0.1</p>\n<p>Other Common Violations:</p>\n<ul>\n<li>object.getProperty().getSubProperty().doSomething()</li>\n<li>user.getAddress().getStreet().getNumber()</li>\n<li>order.getCustomer().getPayment().charge()</li>\n</ul>"},{"q":"How do you handle cross-cutting concerns in object-oriented design?","g":[],"a":"<p>Cross-cutting concerns are aspects of a program that affect multiple modules and cannot be cleanly decomposed from the rest of the system. Examples include logging, security, caching, error handling, and performance monitoring.</p>\n<p>Common Approaches:</p>\n<ol>\n<li>Decorator Pattern</li>\n<li>Aspect-Oriented Programming (AOP)</li>\n<li>Proxy Pattern</li>\n<li>Middleware Pattern</li>\n<li>Event-Driven Architecture</li>\n</ol>\n<p>JavaScript Examples:</p>\n<p>javascript<br>// 1. Decorator Pattern for Logging<br>class Logger {<br>static log(target, propertyName, descriptor) {<br>const originalMethod = descriptor.value;</p>\n<p>descriptor.value = function(...args) {<br>console.log(<code>Calling ${propertyName} with args:</code>, args);<br>const result = originalMethod.apply(this, args);<br>console.log(<code>${propertyName} returned:</code>, result);<br>return result;<br>};</p>\n<p>return descriptor;<br>}<br>}</p>\n<p>class UserService {<br>@Logger.log<br>createUser(userData) {<br>return { id: 1, ...userData };<br>}</p>\n<p>@Logger.log<br>deleteUser(userId) {<br>return <code>User ${userId} deleted</code>;<br>}<br>}</p>\n<p>// 2. Proxy Pattern for Caching<br>class DataService {<br>constructor() {<br>this.cache = new Map();<br>}</p>\n<p>expensiveOperation(id) {<br>console.log(<code>Performing expensive operation for ${id}</code>);<br>return <code>Result for ${id}</code>;<br>}<br>}</p>\n<p>class CachingProxy {<br>constructor(dataService) {<br>this.dataService = dataService;<br>this.cache = new Map();<br>}</p>\n<p>expensiveOperation(id) {<br>if (this.cache.has(id)) {<br>console.log(<code>Cache hit for ${id}</code>);<br>return this.cache.get(id);<br>}</p>\n<p>const result = this.dataService.expensiveOperation(id);<br>this.cache.set(id, result);<br>return result;<br>}<br>}</p>\n<p>// 3. Middleware Pattern<br>class MiddlewareManager {<br>constructor() {<br>this.middlewares = [];<br>}</p>\n<p>use(middleware) {<br>this.middlewares.push(middleware);<br>}</p>\n<p>async execute(context) {<br>let index = 0;</p>\n<p>const next = async () =&gt; {<br>if (index &lt; this.middlewares.length) {<br>const middleware = this.middlewares[index++];<br>await middleware(context, next);<br>}<br>};</p>\n<p>await next();<br>}<br>}</p>\n<p>// Middleware functions<br>const loggingMiddleware = async (context, next) =&gt; {<br>console.log(<code>Request: ${context.method} ${context.path}</code>);<br>await next();<br>console.log(<code>Response: ${context.status}</code>);<br>};</p>\n<p>const authMiddleware = async (context, next) =&gt; {<br>if (!context.headers.authorization) {<br>context.status = 401;<br>return;<br>}<br>await next();<br>};</p>\n<p>const manager = new MiddlewareManager();<br>manager.use(loggingMiddleware);<br>manager.use(authMiddleware);</p>\n<p>Python Examples:</p>\n<p>python<br>import functools<br>import time<br>from typing import Any, Callable</p>\n<h1>1. Decorator Pattern for Timing and Logging</h1>\n<p>def timing_decorator(func):<br>@functools.wraps(func)<br>def wrapper(*args, **kwargs):<br>start_time = time.time()<br>print(f&quot;Starting {func.<strong>name</strong>}&quot;)<br>result = func(*args, **kwargs)<br>end_time = time.time()<br>print(f&quot;{func.<strong>name</strong>} took {end_time - start_time:.2f} seconds&quot;)<br>return result<br>return wrapper</p>\n<p>def logging_decorator(func):<br>@functools.wraps(func)<br>def wrapper(*args, **kwargs):<br>print(f&quot;Calling {func.<strong>name</strong>} with args: {args}, kwargs: {kwargs}&quot;)<br>result = func(*args, **kwargs)<br>print(f&quot;{func.<strong>name</strong>} returned: {result}&quot;)<br>return result<br>return wrapper</p>\n<h1>Combining multiple decorators</h1>\n<p>class UserService:<br>@timing_decorator<br>@logging_decorator<br>def create_user(self, user_data):</p>\n<h1>Simulate some work</h1>\n<p>time.sleep(0.1)<br>return {&quot;id&quot;: 1, **user_data}</p>\n<h1>2. Context Manager for Resource Management</h1>\n<p>class DatabaseTransaction:<br>def <strong>enter</strong>(self):<br>print(&quot;Starting transaction&quot;)<br>return self</p>\n<p>def <strong>exit</strong>(self, exc_type, exc_val, exc_tb):<br>if exc_type:<br>print(&quot;Rolling back transaction&quot;)<br>else:<br>print(&quot;Committing transaction&quot;)<br>return False</p>\n<p>def execute(self, query):<br>print(f&quot;Executing: {query}&quot;)</p>\n<h1>Usage</h1>\n<p>with DatabaseTransaction() as tx:<br>tx.execute(&quot;INSERT INTO users...&quot;)<br>tx.execute(&quot;UPDATE profiles...&quot;)</p>\n<h1>3. Event-Driven Architecture</h1>\n<p>class EventBus:<br>def <strong>init</strong>(self):<br>self.listeners = {}</p>\n<p>def subscribe(self, event_type, handler):<br>if event_type not in self.listeners:<br>self.listeners[event_type] = []<br>self.listeners[event_type].append(handler)</p>\n<p>def publish(self, event_type, data):<br>if event_type in self.listeners:<br>for handler in self.listeners[event_type]:<br>handler(data)</p>\n<h1>Cross-cutting concern handlers</h1>\n<p>class AuditHandler:<br>def handle(self, data):<br>print(f&quot;AUDIT: {data}&quot;)</p>\n<p>class NotificationHandler:<br>def handle(self, data):<br>print(f&quot;NOTIFICATION: {data}&quot;)</p>\n<h1>Setup</h1>\n<p>event_bus = EventBus()<br>audit_handler = AuditHandler()<br>notification_handler = NotificationHandler()</p>\n<p>event_bus.subscribe(&#39;user_created&#39;, audit_handler.handle)<br>event_bus.subscribe(&#39;user_created&#39;, notification_handler.handle)</p>\n<h1>Business logic</h1>\n<p>class UserService:<br>def <strong>init</strong>(self, event_bus):<br>self.event_bus = event_bus</p>\n<p>def create_user(self, user_data):<br>user = {&quot;id&quot;: 1, **user_data}<br>self.event_bus.publish(&#39;user_created&#39;, user)<br>return user</p>\n<p>Best Practices:</p>\n<ul>\n<li>Keep cross-cutting concerns separate from business logic</li>\n<li>Use consistent patterns across the application</li>\n<li>Make cross-cutting behaviors configurable</li>\n<li>Consider performance impact of additional layers</li>\n<li>Document which concerns are handled where</li>\n</ul>"},{"q":"How do you mock objects effectively for unit testing?","g":[],"a":"<p>Mocking is the practice of creating fake objects that simulate the behavior of real objects in controlled ways during testing. Effective mocking helps isolate the unit under test and makes tests faster, more reliable, and independent.</p>\n<p>Key Principles:</p>\n<ul>\n<li>Mock external dependencies, not the object under test</li>\n<li>Mock at the boundary of your system</li>\n<li>Prefer test doubles over real objects for external services</li>\n<li>Keep mocks simple and focused</li>\n<li>Verify interactions when behavior matters</li>\n</ul>\n<p>JavaScript Examples:</p>\n<p>javascript<br>// Using Jest framework</p>\n<p>// 1. Locking</strong>: Check condition, acquire lock, check again</li>\n<li><strong>Singleton Pattern</strong>: Ensure only one instance exists</li>\n<li><strong>Thread-Local Storage</strong>: Each thread gets its own instance</li>\n<li><strong>Memoization</strong>: Cache computed results</li>\n</ol>\n<p><strong>Performance Considerations:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li>Minimize lock contention</li>\n<li>Use read-write locks when appropriate</li>\n<li>Consider lock-free alternatives</li>\n<li>Profile and measure performance impact</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>Common Pitfalls:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li>Broken double-checked locking (memory model issues)</li>\n<li>Race conditions during initialization</li>\n<li>Deadlocks with nested locks</li>\n<li>Memory leaks in caching mechanisms</li>\n</ul>"},{"q":"How would you design a plugin architecture using OOP principles?","g":[],"a":"<p>A plugin architecture allows extending application functionality without modifying core code. Key components include:</p>\n<p><strong>Core Design Elements:</strong></p>\n<ol>\n<li><strong>Plugin Interface/Abstract Base Class</strong>: Defines contract for all plugins</li>\n<li><strong>Plugin Manager</strong>: Handles plugin discovery, loading, and lifecycle</li>\n<li><strong>Registry Pattern</strong>: Maintains loaded plugins and routing</li>\n<li><strong>Event System</strong>: Enables communication between core and plugins</li>\n</ol>\n<p><strong>JavaScript Implementation:</strong></p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div class=\"code-head\"><span>javascript</span><button class=\"copy\" code</li>\n<li><strong>Modularity</strong>: Plugins are self-contained and testable</li>\n<li><strong>Loose Coupling</strong>: Core application doesn&#39;t depend on specific plugins</li>\n<li><strong>Runtime Configuration</strong>: Load/unload plugins dynamically</li>\n</ul>"},{"q":"Explain the difference between invariants, preconditions, and postconditions.","g":[],"a":"<p>These are fundamental concepts in Design by Contract that help ensure program correctness:</p>\n<p><strong>Preconditions:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li>Conditions that must be true when a method/function is called</li>\n<li>Caller&#39;s responsibility to ensure</li>\n<li>Define what the method expects as input</li>\n<li>If violated, indicates a bug in the calling code</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>Postconditions:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li>Conditions that must be true when a method/function returns</li>\n<li>Method&#39;s responsibility to ensure</li>\n<li>Define what the method guarantees as output</li>\n<li>If violated, indicates a bug in the method implementation</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>Invariants:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li>Conditions that must always be true for an object/class</li>\n<li>Must hold before and after every public method call</li>\n<li>Define the consistent state of an object</li>\n<li>Class designer&#39;s responsibility to maintain</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>JavaScript Implementation:</strong></p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div class=\"code-head\"><span>javascript</span><button class=\"copy\" type=\"button\">Copy</button></div><pre><code class=\"hljs\"><i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>BankAccount</i> {\n  <i class=f>constructor</i>(<i class=p>initialBalance = <i class=n>0</i></i>) {\n    <i (!condition) {\n        <i class=k>throw</i> <i class=k>new</i> <i class=f>Error</i>(<i class=s>`Postcondition violation in <i class=u>${methodName}</i>: <i class=u>${message}</i>`</i>);\n      }\n    }\n  }\n  \n  <i class=f>deposit</i>(<i class=p>amount</i>) {\n    <i class=c>// Store old state for postcondition checking</i>\n    <i class=k>const</i> oldBalance = <i class=v>this</i>.<i     <i class=v>self</i>._width = width\n        <i class=v>self</i>._height = height\n        <i class=v>self</i>._check_invariants()\n    \n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>_check_invariants</i>(<i class=p>self</i>):\n        <i class=k>if</i> <i class=v>self</i>._width &lt;= <i class=n>0</i> <i class=k>or</i> <i class=v>self</i>._height &lt;= <i class=n>0</i>:\n            <i class=k>raise</i> class=s>&quot;Width must be positive&quot;</i></i>)</i>\n<i class=m>    @ensure(<i class=p><i class=k>lambda</i> result, self, w: self._width == w, <i class=s>&quot;Width must be set class=v>self</i>._height</code></pre></div>\n<p><strong>Key Differences Summary:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li><strong>Preconditions</strong>: What caller must ensure (input validation)</li>\n<li><strong>Postconditions</strong>: What method must ensure (output validation)</li>\n<li><strong>Invariants</strong>: What object must always maintain (state consistency)</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>Benefits:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li><strong>Early Bug Detection</strong>: Catch issues at the boundary</li>\n<li><strong>Clear Documentation</strong>: Contracts serve as executable documentation</li>\n<li><strong>Debugging Aid</strong>: Pinpoint exactly where contracts are violated</li>\n<li><strong>Testing Support</strong>: Natural test cases from contract conditions</li>\n</ul>"},{"q":"What is aspect-oriented programming and how does it relate to OOP?","g":[],"a":"<p>Aspect-Oriented Programming (AOP) is a programming paradigm that complements OOP by separating cross-cutting concerns from core business logic. While OOP organizes code around objects and their interactions, AOP focuses on concerns that span multiple classes like logging, security, caching, and transaction management.</p>\n<p><strong>Key Concepts:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li><strong>Aspects</strong>: Modular units of cross-cutting functionality</li>\n<li><strong>Join Points</strong>: Points in program execution where aspects can be applied</li>\n<li><strong>Advice</strong>: Code that runs at join points (before, after, around)</li>\n<li><strong>Pointcuts</strong>: Expressions that define where advice should be applied</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>JavaScript Implementation:</strong></p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div class=\"code-head\"><span>javascript</span><button class=\"copy\" type=\"button\">Copy</button></div><pre><code class=\"hljs\"><i class=c>// Decorator pattern for AOP in JavaScript</i>\n<i class=k>function</i> <i class=f>logMethod</i>(<i class=p>target, propertyName, descriptor</i>) {\n  <i class=k>const</i> method = descriptor.<i class=v>value</i>;\n  \n  descriptor.<i class=v>value</i> = <i class=k>function</i> (<i class=p>...args</i>) {\n    <i class=v>console</i>.<i class=f>log</i>(<i class=s>`Calling <i class=u>${propertyName}</i> with args:`</i>, args);\n    <i class=k>const</i> result = method.<i class=f>apply</i>(<i class=v>this</i>, args);\n    <i class=v>console</i>.<i class=f>log</i>(<i class=s>`<i class=u>${propertyName}</i> returned:`</i>, result);\n    <i class=k>return</i> result;\n  };\n  \n  <i class=k>return</i> descriptor;\n}\n\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>Calculator</i> {\n  @logMethod\n  <i class=f>add</i>(<i class=p>a, b</i>) {\n    <i class=k>return</i> a + b;\n  }\n  \n  @logMethod\n  <i class=f>multiply</i>(<i class=p>a, b</i>) {\n    <i class=k>return</i> a * b;\n  }\n}\n\n<i class=c>// Proxy-based AOP</i>\n<i class=k>function</i> <i class=f>createAOPProxy</i>(<i class=p>target, aspects</i>) {\n  <i class=k>return</i> <i class=k>new</i> <i class=f>Proxy</i>(target, {\n    <i class=f>get</i>(<i class=p>obj, prop</i>) {\n      <i class=k>if</i> (<i class=k>typeof</i> obj[prop] === <i class=s>&#x27;function&#x27;</i>) {\n        <i class=k>return</i> <i class=k>function</i> (<i class=p>...args</i>) {\n          <i class=c>// Before advice</i>\n          aspects.<i class=v>before</i>?.(prop, args);\n          \n          <i class=k>const</i> result = obj[prop].<i class=f>apply</i>(obj, args);\n          \n          <i class=c>// After advice</i>\n          aspects.<i class=v>after</i>?.(prop, result);\n          \n          <i class=k>return</i> result;\n        };\n      }\n      <i class=k>return</i> obj[prop];\n    }\n  });\n}\n\n<i class=k>const</i> calc = <i class=k>new</i> <i class=f>Calculator</i>();\n<i class=k>const</i> proxiedCalc = <i class=f>createAOPProxy</i>(calc, {\n  <i class=a>before</i>: <i class=function>(<i class=p>method, args</i>) =&gt;</i> <i class=v>console</i>.<i class=f>log</i>(<i class=s>`Before <i class=u>${method}</i>:`</i>, args),\n  <i class=a>after</i>: <i class=function>(<i class=p>method, result</i>) =&gt;</i> <i class=v>console</i>.<i class=f>log</i>(<i class=s>`After <i class=u>${method}</i>:`</i>, result)\n});</code></pre></div>\n<p><strong>Python Implementation:</strong></p>\n<div time\n\n<i class=c># Decorator-based AOP</i>\n<i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>log_execution</i>(<i class=p>func</i>):\n<i class=m>    @functools.wraps(<i class=p>func</i>)</i>\n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>wrapper</i>(<i class=p>*args, **kwargs</i>):\n        <i class=b>print</i>(<i class=s>f&quot;Executing <i class=u>{func.__name__}</i> with args: <i class=u>{args}</i>, kwargs: <i     <i class=k>return</i> result\n    <i class=k>return</i> wrapper\n\n<i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>measure_time</i>(<i class=p>func</i>):\n<i class=m>    @functools.wraps(<i class=p>func</i>)</i>\n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>wrapper</i>(<i class=p>*args, **kwargs</i>):\n        start = time.time()\n        result = func(*args, **kwargs)\n        end = time.time()\n        <i bases, dct</i>):\n        <i class=k>for</i> key, value <i class=k>in</i> dct.items():\n            <i class=k>if</i> <i class=b>callable</i>(value) <i class=k>and</i> <i class=k>not</i> primary concerns (business logic, data encapsulation)</li>\n<li>AOP handles secondary concerns (logging, security, caching)</li>\n<li>Together they provide better separation of concerns and cleaner code</li>\n<li>AOP reduces code duplication for cross-cutting functionality</li>\n</ul>"},{"q":"How do you implement design by contract in OOP?","g":[],"a":"<p>Design by Contract (DbC) is a software design approach where components define formal, precise, and verifiable interface specifications. It uses preconditions, postconditions, and invariants to ensure program correctness.</p>\n<p><strong>Key Components:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li><strong>Preconditions</strong>: What must be true when a method is called</li>\n<li><strong>Postconditions</strong>: What must be true when a method returns</li>\n<li><strong>Class Invariants</strong>: Properties that must always hold for an object</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>JavaScript Implementation:</strong></p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div class=\"code-head\"><span>javascript</span><button class=\"copy\" type=\"button\">Copy</button></div><pre><code class=\"hljs\"><i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>ContractError</i> <i class=k>extends</i> <i class=f>Error</i> {\n  <i class=f>constructor</i>(<i class=p>type, message</i>) {\n    <i class=v>super</i>(<i class=s>`<i class=u>${type}</i>: <i class=u>${message}</i>`</i>);\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>type</i> = type;\n  }\n}\n\n<i class=k>function</i> <i class=f>requires</i>(<i class=p>condition, message</i>) {\n  <i class=k>if</i> (!condition) {\n    <i class=k>throw</i> <i class=k>new</i> <i class=f>ContractError</i>(<i message);\n  }\n}\n\n<i class=c>// Contract decorator</i>\n<i class=k>function</i> <i class=f>contract</i>(<i class=p>preconditions = [], postconditions = []</i>) {\n  <i class=k>return</i> <i postconditions</i>\n      postconditions.<i class=f>forEach</i>(<i class=function><i class=p>post</i> =&gt;</i> {\n        <i class=f>ensures</i>(post.<i class=f>condition</i>(result, oldState, ...args), post.<i class=v>message</i>);\n      });\n      \n      <i class=k>return</i> result;\n    };\n    \n    <i class=k>return</i> descriptor;\n  };\n}\n\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=k>return</i> <i class=v>self</i>._balance\n\n<i class=c># Usage example with assertions</i>\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>Stack</i>:\n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>__init__</i>(<i invariant(<i class=b>isinstance</i>(<i class=v>self</i>._items, <i class=b>list</i>), <i class=s>&quot;Items must be stored in a list&quot;</i>)\n    \n    <i class=k>def</i> <i     ]\n    </i>)</i>\n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>push</i>(<i class=p>self, item</i>):\n        <i class=v>self</i>._items.append(item)\n        <i class=v>self</i>._check_invariant()\n    \n<i postconditions=[\n            <i class=k>lambda</i> self, result, old_state: <i class=b>len</i>(<i class=p>self._items</i>) == old_state[<i class=s>&#x27;size&#x27;</i>] - <i class=n>1</i>\n        ]\n    </i>)</i>\n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>pop</i>(<i class=p>self</i>):\n        result = <i class=v>self</i>._items.pop()\n        <i class=v>self</i>._check_invariant()\n        <i class=k>return</i> result</code></pre></div>\n<p><strong>Benefits:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li><strong>Documentation</strong>: Contracts serve as executable documentation</li>\n<li><strong>Testing</strong>: Automatic verification of correctness</li>\n<li><strong>Debugging</strong>: Early detection of logic errors</li>\n<li><strong>Reliability</strong>: Ensures components behave as expected</li>\n<li><strong>Maintenance</strong>: Makes code changes safer</li>\n</ul>"},{"q":"What are the challenges of testing polymorphic code?","g":[],"a":"<p>Testing polymorphic code presents unique challenges because the same interface can have multiple implementations, and the actual behavior depends on the runtime type. This requires comprehensive testing strategies to ensure all possible code paths work correctly.</p>\n<p><strong>Key Challenges:</strong></p>\n<ol>\n<li><strong>Multiple Implementation Coverage</strong>: Need to test all concrete implementations</li>\n<li><strong>Runtime Type Resolution</strong>: Behavior determined at runtime, not compile time</li>\n<li><strong>Interface Contract Verification</strong>: All implementations must honor the same contract</li>\n<li><strong>Complex Inheritance Hierarchies</strong>: Deep inheritance can create unexpected behaviors</li>\n<li><strong>Dynamic Dispatch</strong>: Method calls resolved at runtime</li>\n</ol>\n<p><strong>JavaScript Implementation:</strong></p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div !== expected) {\n      <i class=k>throw</i> <i class=k>new</i> <i class=f>Error</i>(<i class=s>`<i class=u>${message}</i>: expected <i class=u>${expected}</i>, got <i class=u>${actual}</i>`</i>);\n    }\n    <i class=v>console</i>.<i class=f>log</i>(<i class=s>`✓ <i class=u>${message}</i>`</i>);\n  }\n  \n  <i class=f>runAllTests</i>(<i class=p></i>) {\n    <i class=v>console</i>.<i requirements</li>\n</ol>"},{"q":"Explain the concept of object pooling and when to use it.","g":[],"a":"<p>Object pooling is a design pattern that maintains a collection of reusable objects to avoid expensive object creation and garbage collection. Instead of creating and destroying objects repeatedly, you reuse existing ones from a pool.</p>\n<p><strong>When to use:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li>Creating objects is expensive (database connections, threads, heavy objects)</li>\n<li>Frequent object creation causes garbage collection pressure</li>\n<li>Objects have significant initialization overhead</li>\n<li>Memory allocation/deallocation is costly</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>JavaScript Example:</strong></p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div class=\"code-head\"><span>javascript</span><button class=\"copy\" type=\"button\">Copy</button></div><pre><code class=\"hljs\"><i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>ObjectPool</i> {\n  <i class=f>constructor</i>(<i class=p>createFn, resetFn, maxSize = <i class=n>10</i></i>) {\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>createFn</i> = createFn;\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>resetFn</i> = resetFn;\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>pool</i> = [];\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>maxSize</i> = maxSize;\n  }\n\n  <i class=f>acquire</i>(<i class=p></i>) {\n    <i class=k>if</i> (<i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>pool</i>.<i class=v>length</i> &gt; <i class=n>0</i>) {\n      <i class=k>return</i> <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>pool</i>.<i class=f>pop</i>();\n    }\n    <i class=k>return</i> <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=f>createFn</i>();\n  }\n\n  <i class=f>release</i>(<i class=p>obj</i>) {\n    <i class=k>if</i> (<i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>pool</i>.<i class=v>length</i> &lt; <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>maxSize</i>) {\n      <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=f>resetFn</i>(obj);\n      <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>pool</i>.<i class=f>push</i>(obj);\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n<i class=c>// Usage example with expensive objects</i>\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>ExpensiveObject</i> {\n  <i class=f>constructor</i>(<i class=p></i>) {\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>data</i> = <i class=k>new</i> <i class=f>Array</i>(<i class=n>1000</i>).<i class=f>fill</i>(<i class=n>0</i>);\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>processed</i> = <i class=l>false</i>;\n  }\n\n  <i class=f>process</i>(<i class=p></i>) {\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>processed</i> = <i class=l>true</i>;\n    <i class=c>// Heavy computation</i>\n  }\n\n  <i class=f>reset</i>(<i class=p></i>) {\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>processed</i> = <i class=l>false</i>;\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>data</i>.<i class=f>fill</i>(<i class=n>0</i>);\n  }\n}\n\n<i class=k>const</i> pool = <i class=k>new</i> <i class=f>ObjectPool</i>(\n  <i class=function>() =&gt;</i> <i class=k>new</i> <i class=f>ExpensiveObject</i>(),\n  <i class=function>(<i class=p>obj</i>) =&gt;</i> obj.<i class=f>reset</i>()\n);\n\n<i class=c>// Instead of: new ExpensiveObject()</i>\n<i class=k>const</i> obj = pool.<i class=f>acquire</i>();\nobj.<i class=f>process</i>();\npool.<i class=f>release</i>(obj); <i class=c>// Reuse instead of GC</i></code></pre></div>\n<p><strong>Python Example:</strong></p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div class=\"code-head\"><span>python</span><button class=\"copy\" type=\"button\">Copy</button></div><pre><code class=\"hljs\"><i class=k>from</i> queue <i class=k>import</i> Queue\n<i class=k>import</i> threading\n\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>ObjectPool</i>:\n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>__init__</i>(<i class=p>self, create_fn, reset_fn, max_size=<i class=n>10</i></i>):\n        <i class=v>self</i>.create_fn = create_fn\n        <i class=v>self</i>.reset_fn = reset_fn\n        <i class=v>self</i>.pool = Queue(maxsize=max_size)\n        <i class=v>self</i>.max_size = max_size\n        <i class=v>self</i>._lock = threading.Lock()\n    \n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>acquire</i>(<i class=p>self</i>):\n        <i class=k>try</i>:\n            <i class=k>return</i> <i class=v>self</i>.pool.get_nowait()\n        <i class=k>except</i>:\n            <i class=k>return</i> <i class=v>self</i>.create_fn()\n    \n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>release</i>(<i class=p>self, obj</i>):\n        <i class=v>self</i>.reset_fn(obj)\n        <i class=k>try</i>:\n            <i class=v>self</i>.pool.put_nowait(obj)\n        <i class=k>except</i>:\n            <i class=k>pass</i>  <i class=c># Pool is full, let GC handle it</i>\n\n<i class=c># Database connection pool example</i>\n<i class=k>import</i> sqlite3\n\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>DatabasePool</i>:\n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>__init__</i>(<i class=p>self, db_path, pool_size=<i class=n>5</i></i>):\n        <i class=v>self</i>.db_path = db_path\n        <i class=v>self</i>.pool = Queue(maxsize=pool_size)\n        <i class=c># Pre-populate pool</i>\n        <i class=k>for</i> _ <i class=k>in</i> <i class=b>range</i>(pool_size):\n            conn = sqlite3.connect(db_path)\n            <i class=v>self</i>.pool.put(conn)\n    \n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>get_connection</i>(<i class=p>self</i>):\n        <i class=k>return</i> <i class=v>self</i>.pool.get()\n    \n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>return_connection</i>(<i class=p>self, conn</i>):\n        <i class=c># Reset connection state</i>\n        conn.rollback()\n        <i class=v>self</i>.pool.put(conn)\n    \n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>close_all</i>(<i class=p>self</i>):\n        <i class=k>while</i> <i class=k>not</i> <i class=v>self</i>.pool.empty():\n            conn = <i class=v>self</i>.pool.get()\n            conn.close()\n\n<i class=c># Usage</i>\ndb_pool = DatabasePool(<i class=s>&#x27;app.db&#x27;</i>)\nconn = db_pool.get_connection()\n<i class=c># Use connection</i>\ndb_pool.return_connection(conn)</code></pre></div>\n<p><strong>Benefits:</strong> Reduced GC pressure, consistent performance, resource reuse<br><strong>Drawbacks:</strong> Memory overhead, complexity, potential memory leaks if not managed properly</p>"},{"q":"What are the performance implications of virtual function calls?","g":[],"a":"<p>Virtual function calls have performance overhead compared to direct function calls due to dynamic dispatch through virtual tables (vtables). The runtime must look up the correct function implementation based on the object&#39;s actual type.</p>\n<p><strong>Performance Implications:</strong></p>\n<ol>\n<li><strong>Indirection Overhead:</strong> Extra memory access to vtable</li>\n<li><strong>Cache Misses:</strong> Vtable lookups can cause CPU cache misses</li>\n<li><strong>Inlining Prevention:</strong> Compiler can&#39;t inline virtual calls at compile time</li>\n<li><strong>Branch Prediction:</strong> Harder for CPU to predict which function will be called</li>\n</ol>\n<p><strong>JavaScript Example (Prototype Chain Lookup):</strong></p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div class=\"code-head\"><span>javascript</span><button class=\"copy\" type=\"button\">Copy</button></div><pre><code class=\"hljs\"><i class=c>// Direct method call - faster</i>\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>FastClass</i> {\n  <i class=f>calculate</i>(<i class=p>x, y</i>) {\n    <i class=k>return</i> x + y;\n  }\n}\n\n<i class=c>// Polymorphic approach - slower due to prototype chain lookup</i>\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>BaseCalculator</i> {\n  <i class=f>calculate</i>(<i class=p>x, y</i>) {\n    <i class=k>throw</i> <i class=k>new</i> <i class=f>Error</i>(<i class=s>&#x27;Must implement&#x27;</i>);\n  }\n}\n\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>AddCalculator</i> <i class=k>extends</i> <i class=f>BaseCalculator</i> {\n  <i class=f>calculate</i>(<i class=p>x, y</i>) {\n    <i class=k>return</i> x + y;\n  }\n}\n\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>MultiplyCalculator</i> <i class=k>extends</i> <i class=f>BaseCalculator</i> {\n  <i class=f>calculate</i>(<i class=p>x, y</i>) {\n    <i class=k>return</i> x * y;\n  }\n}\n\n<i class=c>// Performance comparison</i>\n<i class=k>function</i> <i class=f>benchmarkDirect</i>(<i class=p></i>) {\n  <i class=k>const</i> calc = <i class=k>new</i> <i class=f>FastClass</i>();\n  <i class=k>let</i> result = <i class=n>0</i>;\n  \n  <i class=v>console</i>.<i class=f>time</i>(<i class=s>&#x27;Direct calls&#x27;</i>);\n  <i class=k>for</i> (<i class=k>let</i> i = <i class=n>0</i>; i &lt; <i class=n>1000000</i>; i++) {\n    result += calc.<i class=f>calculate</i>(i, i + <i class=n>1</i>);\n  }\n  <i class=v>console</i>.<i class=f>timeEnd</i>(<i class=s>&#x27;Direct calls&#x27;</i>);\n  <i class=k>return</i> result;\n}\n\n<i class=k>function</i> <i class=f>benchmarkPolymorphic</i>(<i class=p></i>) {\n  <i class=k>const</i> calculators = [\n    <i class=k>new</i> <i class=f>AddCalculator</i>(),\n    <i class=k>new</i> <i class=f>MultiplyCalculator</i>()\n  ];\n  <i class=k>let</i> result = <i class=n>0</i>;\n  \n  <i class=v>console</i>.<i class=f>time</i>(<i class=s>&#x27;Polymorphic calls&#x27;</i>);\n  <i class=k>for</i> (<i class=k>let</i> i = <i class=n>0</i>; i &lt; <i class=n>1000000</i>; i++) {\n    <i class=k>const</i> calc = calculators[i % <i class=n>2</i>];\n    result += calc.<i class=f>calculate</i>(i, i + <i class=n>1</i>);\n  }\n  <i class=v>console</i>.<i class=f>timeEnd</i>(<i class=s>&#x27;Polymorphic calls&#x27;</i>);\n  <i class=k>return</i> result;\n}\n\n<i class=c>// Optimization: Function caching</i>\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>OptimizedCalculator</i> {\n  <i class=f>constructor</i>(<i class=p></i>) {\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>cachedMethod</i> = <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>calculate</i>.<i class=f>bind</i>(<i class=v>this</i>);\n  }\n  \n  <i class=f>calculate</i>(<i class=p>x, y</i>) {\n    <i class=k>return</i> x + y;\n  }\n  \n  <i class=f>fastCalculate</i>(<i class=p>x, y</i>) {\n    <i class=k>return</i> <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=f>cachedMethod</i>(x, y);\n  }\n}</code></pre></div>\n<p><strong>Python Example:</strong></p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div class=\"code-head\"><span>python</span><button class=\"copy\" type=\"button\">Copy</button></div><pre><code class=\"hljs\"><i class=k>import</i> time\n<i class=k>from</i> abc <i class=k>import</i> ABC, abstractmethod\n\n<i class=c># Direct calls - faster</i>\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>DirectCalculator</i>:\n    <i class=k>def</i> <i <i class=f>MultiplyCalculator</i>(<i class=f>AbstractCalculator</i>):\n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>calculate</i>(<i class=p>self, x, y</i>):\n        <i class=k>return</i> x * y\n\n<i actual type can be determined at compile time.</p>\n<p><strong>When Devirtualization Occurs:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li>Final/sealed classes or methods</li>\n<li>Whole program optimization can see all implementations</li>\n<li>Type is provably known at compile time</li>\n<li>Single implementation exists in the program</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>JavaScript Example:</strong></p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div class=\"code-head\"><span>javascript</span><button class=\"copy\" type=\"button\">Copy</button></div><pre><code class=\"hljs\"><i class=c>// JavaScript engines perform similar   <i class=k>for</i> i <i class=k>in</i> <i class=b>range</i>(<i class=n>100000</i>):\n        p = factory.create_particle(i, i, <i class=n>1</i>, <i class=n>1</i>)\n        particles.append(p)\n    \n    <i class=k>for</i> p <i class=k>in</i> particles:\n        factory.destroy_particle(p)\n    \n    pooled_time = time.time() - start_time\n    <i class=b>print</i>(<i class=s>f&quot;With pooling:   \n    no_pool_time = time.time() - start_time\n    <i class=b>print</i>(<i class=s>f&quot;Without pooling: <i class=u>{no_pool_time:<i class=n>.4</i>f}</i>s&quot;</i>)\n\n<i class=k>if</i> __name__ == <i class=s>&quot;__main__&quot;</i>:\n    benchmark_creation()</code></pre></div>\n<p><strong>Key Optimization Techniques:</strong></p>\n<ol>\n<li><strong>Object Pooling</strong>: Reuse objects to avoid allocation/deallocation overhead</li>\n<li><strong>Batch Allocation</strong>: Allocate multiple objects at once</li>\n<li><strong>Flyweight Pattern</strong>: Share immutable data between objects</li>\n<li><strong>Pre-allocation</strong>: Reserve memory upfront to avoid dynamic allocation</li>\n<li><strong>Memory Layout</strong>: Use <strong>slots</strong> in Python or similar techniques for cache efficiency</li>\n<li><strong>Factory Caching</strong>: Cache expensive-to-create objects</li>\n</ol>"},{"q":"What is object pooling and when should you use it?","g":[],"a":"<p>Object pooling is a design pattern that maintains a pool of reusable objects to minimize the overhead of object creation and garbage collection.</p>\n<p><strong>When to Use Object Pooling:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li>Frequent creation/destruction of expensive objects</li>\n<li>Memory allocation is a performance bottleneck</li>\n<li>Garbage collection pressure needs reduction</li>\n<li>Objects have predictable lifecycle patterns</li>\n<li>Working with limited memory environments</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>JavaScript Example:</strong></p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div class=\"code-head\"><span>javascript</span><button class=\"copy\" type=\"button\">Copy</button></div><pre><code class=\"hljs\"><i class=c>// Generic Object Pool Implementation</i>\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>ObjectPool</i> {\n  <i class=f>constructor</i>(<i class=p>createFn, resetFn, maxSize = <i class=n>100</i></i>) {\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>createFn</i> = createFn;\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>resetFn</i> = resetFn;\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>maxSize</i> = maxSize;\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>pool</i> = [];\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>created</i> = <i class=n>0</i>;\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>acquired</i> = <i class=n>0</i>;\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>released</i> = <i class=n>0</i>;\n  }\n  \n  <i class=f>acquire</i>(<i class=p></i>) {\n    <i class=k>let</i> obj;\n    <i class=k>if</i> (<i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>pool</i>.<i class=v>length</i> &gt; <i class=n>0</i>) {\n      obj = <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>pool</i>.<i class=f>pop</i>();\n    } <i class=k>else</i> {\n      obj = <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=f>createFn</i>();\n      <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>created</i>++;\n    }\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>acquired</i>++;\n    <i class=k>return</i> obj;\n  }\n  \n  <i class=f>release</i>(<i class=p>obj</i>) {\n    <i class=k>if</i> (<i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>pool</i>.<i class=v>length</i> &lt; <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>maxSize</i>) {\n      <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=f>resetFn</i>(obj);\n      <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>pool</i>.<i class=f>push</i>(obj);\n      <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>released</i>++;\n    }\n  }\n  \n  <i class=f>getStats</i>(<i class=p></i>) {\n    <i class=k>return</i> {\n      <i class=a>poolSize</i>: <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>pool</i>.<i class=v>length</i>,\n      <i class=a>created</i>: <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>created</i>,\n      <i class=a>acquired</i>: <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>acquired</i>,\n      <i class=a>released</i>: <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>released</i>,\n      <i class=a>hitRate</i>: <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>released</i> / <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>acquired</i>\n    };\n  }\n}\n\n<i class=c>// Example: DOM Element Pool</i>\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>DOMElementPool</i> <i class=k>extends</i> <i = tagName;\n  }\n}\n\n<i class=c>// Example: Game Object Pool</i>\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>Bullet</i> {\n  <i class=f>constructor</i>(<i class=p></i>) {\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i example with performance monitoring</i>\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>Game</i> {\n  <i class=f>constructor</i>(<i class=p></i>) {\n    <i class=v>this</i>.<i class=v>bulletManager</i> = <i class=v>frameCount</i>++;\n    \n    <i class=c>// Random bullet firing</i>\n    <i class=k>if</i> (<i class=f>Math</i>.<i class=f>random</i>() &lt; <i class=n>0.1</i>) {\n      <i class=v>this</i>.<i }\n}</code></pre></div>\n<p><strong>Python Example:</strong></p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div class=\"code-head\"><span>python</span><button class=\"copy\" type=\"button\">Copy</button></div><pre><code class=\"hljs\"><i class=k>import</i> time\n<i class=k>import</i> threading\n<i class=k>from</i> queue <i class=k>import</i> Queue\n<i class=k>from</i> typing <i class=k>import</i> <i class=t>Callable</i>, TypeVar, <i class=t>Generic</i>, <i class=t>Optional</i>\n<i class=k>from</i> dataclasses <i class=k>import</i> dataclass\n<i class=k>from</i> abc <i class=k>import</i> ABC, abstractmethod\n\nT = TypeVar(<i class=s>&#x27;T&#x27;</i>)\n\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>ObjectPool</i>(<i class=t>Generic</i>[T]):\n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>__init__</i>(<i class=p>self, \n                 factory: <i class=t>Callable</i>[[], T], \n                 reset: <i class=t>Callable</i>[[T], <i class=l>None</i>], \n                 max_size: <i class=b>int</i> = <i class=n>100</i></i>):\n        <i class=v>self</i>.factory = factory\n        <i class=v>self</i>.reset = reset\n        <i class=v>self</i>.max_size = max_size\n        <i class=v>self</i>._pool = Queue(maxsize=max_size)\n        <i class=v>self</i>._lock = threading.Lock()\n        \n        <i class=c># Statistics</i>\n        <i class=v>self</i>.created = <i class=n>0</i>\n        <i class=v>self</i>.acquired = <i class=n>0</i>\n        <i class=v>self</i>.released = <i class=n>0</i>\n        \n        <i class=c># Pre-populate pool</i>\n        <i class=k>for</i> _ <i class=k>in</i> <i class=b>range</i>(<i class=b>min</i>(<i class=n>10</i>, max_size)):\n            <i class=k>try</i>:\n                obj = <i class=v>self</i>.factory()\n                <i class=v>self</i>._pool.put_nowait(obj)\n                <i class=v>self</i>.created += <i class=n>1</i>\n            <i class=k>except</i>:\n                <i class=k>break</i>\n    \n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>acquire</i>(<i class=p>self</i>) -&gt; T:\n        <i class=k>with</i> <i class=v>self</i>._lock:\n            <i class=v>self</i>.acquired += <i class=n>1</i>\n            \n        <i class=k>try</i>:\n            <i class=c># Try to get from pool first</i>\n            obj = <i class=v>self</i>._pool.get_nowait()\n            <i class=k>return</i> obj\n        <i class=k>except</i>:\n            <i class=c># Create new if pool is empty</i>\n            <i class=k>with</i> <i class=v>self</i>._lock:\n                <i class=v>self</i>.created += <i class=n>1</i>\n            <i class=k>return</i> <i class=v>self</i>.factory()\n    \n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>release</i>(<i class=p>self, obj: T</i>) -&gt; <i class=l>None</i>:\n        <i class=k>try</i>:\n            <i class=v>self</i>.reset(obj)\n            <i class=v>self</i>._pool.put_nowait(obj)\n            <i class=k>with</i> <i class=v>self</i>._lock:\n                <i class=v>self</i>.released += <i class=n>1</i>\n        <i class=k>except</i>:\n            <i class=c># Pool is full, let object be garbage collected</i>\n            <i class=k>pass</i>\n    \n    <i <i class=s>&#x27;pool_size&#x27;</i>: <i class=v>self</i>._pool.qsize(),\n                <i class=s>&#x27;created&#x27;</i>: <i class=v>self</i>.created,\n                <i class=s>&#x27;acquired&#x27;</i>: <i class=v>self</i>.acquired,\n                <i class=s>&#x27;released&#x27;</i>: <i class=v>self</i>.released,\n                <i class=s>&#x27;hit_rate&#x27;</i>: <i class=v>self</i>.released / <i class=b>max</i>(<i class=v>self</i>.acquired, <i class=n>1</i>)\n            }\n\n<i class=c># Example: Database Connection Pool</i>\n<i class=m>@dataclass</i>\n<i class=k>class</i> <i class=f>MockConnection</i>:\n    connection_id: <i class=b>int</i>\n    is_active: <i class=b>bool</i> = <i class=l>False</i>\n    last_query_time: <i class=b>float</i> = <i class=n>0</i>\n    \n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>execute_query</i>(<i class=p>self, query: <i class=b>str</i></i>) -&gt; <i class=b>str</i>:\n        <i class=v>self</i>.last_query_time = time.time()\n        <i class=k>return</i> <i class=s>f&quot;Executed: <i class=u>{query[:<i class=n>50</i>]}</i>...&quot;</i>\n    \n    <i class=k>def</i> <i       connection = <i class=v>self</i>.pool.acquire()\n        <i class=k>try</i>:\n            connection.is_active = <i class=l>True</i>\n            result = connection.execute_query(query)\n            <i class=k>return</i> result\n        <i class=k>finally</i>:\n            <i class=v>self</i>.pool.release(connection)\n    \n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>get_stats</i>(<i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>__init__</i>(<i class=p>self</i>):\n        <i class=c># Simulate expensive initialization</i>\n        <i class=v>self</i>.data = [i * i <i class=k>for</i> i <i class=k>in</i> <i class=b>range</i>(<i class=n>10000</i>)]\n        <i class=v>self</i>.computation_cache = {}\n        <i class=v>self</i>.created_at = time.time()\n    \n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>compute</i>(<i class=p>self, n: <i class=b>int</i></i>) -&gt; <i class=b>int</i>:\n        <i class=k>if</i> n <i class=k>not</i> <i class=k>in</i> <i class=v>self</i>.computation_cache:\n            <i class=c># Expensive computation</i>\n            result = <i class=b>sum</i>(i * i <i class=k>for</i> i <i class=k>in</i> <i class=b>range</i>(n))\n            <i class=v>self</i>.computation_cache[n] = result\n        <i class=k>return</i> <i class=v>self</i>.computation_cache[n]\n    \n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>reset</i>(<i class=p>self</i>):\n        <i class=v>self</i>.computation_cache.clear()\n\n<i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>benchmark_object_pooling</i>():\n    <i class=c># Setup pools</i>\n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>create_resource</i>():\n        <i class=k>return</i> ExpensiveResource()\n    \n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>reset_resource</i>(<i class=p>resource</i>):\n        resource.reset()\n    \n    pool = ObjectPool(create_resource, reset_resource, <i class=n>10</i>)\n    \n    <i class=c># Benchmark with pooling</i>\n    start_time = time.time()\n    with_pool_results = []\n    \n    <i class=k>for</i> i <i class=k>in</i> <i class=b>range</i>(<i class=n>1000</i>):\n        resource = pool.acquire()\n        <i class=k>try</i>:\n            result = resource.compute(<i class=n>100</i>)\n            with_pool_results.append(result)\n        <i class=k>finally</i>:\n            pool.release(resource)\n    \n    pool_time = time.time() - start_time\n    <i class=b>print</i>(<i class=s>f&quot;With pool: <i class=u>{pool_time:<i class=n>.4</i>f}</i>s&quot;</i>)\n    \n    <i class=c># Benchmark without pooling</i>\n    start_time = time.time()\n    without_pool_results = []\n    \n    <i class=k>for</i> i <i class=k>in</i> <i class=b>range</i>(<i class=n>1000</i>):\n        resource = ExpensiveResource()\n        result = resource.compute(<i class=n>100</i>)\n        without_pool_results.append(result)\n        <i class=c># Let GC handle cleanup</i>\n    \n    no_pool_time = time.time() - start_time\n    <i class=b>print</i>(<i class=s>f&quot;Without pool: <i class=u>{no_pool_time:<i class=n>.4</i>f}</i>s&quot;</i>)\n    <i class=b>print</i>(<i class=s>f&quot;Pool stats: <i class=u>{pool.get_stats()}</i>&quot;</i>)\n    \n    <i class=k>return</i> pool_time, no_pool_time\n\n<i class=c># Thread-safe usage example</i>\n<i class=k>import</i> concurrent.futures\n\n<i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>worker_function</i>(<i class=p>pool: ObjectPool, task_id: <i class=b>int</i></i>) -&gt; <i class=b>str</i>:\n    resource = pool.acquire()\n    <i class=k>try</i>:\n        <i class=c># Simulate work</i>\n        result = resource.compute(task_id % <i class=n>1000</i>)\n        time.sleep(<i class=n>0.001</i>)  <i class=c># Simulate processing time</i>\n        <i class=k>return</i> <i class=s>f&quot;Task <i class=u>{task_id}</i>: <i class=u>{result}</i>&quot;</i>\n    <i class=k>finally</i>:\n        pool.release(resource)\n\n<i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>test_concurrent_usage</i>():\n    pool = ObjectPool(\n        <i class=k>lambda</i>: ExpensiveResource(),\n        <i class=k>lambda</i> r: r.reset(),\n        <i class=n>5</i>  <i class=c># Small pool to test contention</i>\n    )\n    \n    <i class=c># Run concurrent tasks</i>\n    <i class=k>with</i> concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=<i class=n>10</i>) <i class=k>as</i> executor:\n        futures = [executor.submit(worker_function, pool, i) <i class=k>for</i> i <i class=k>in</i> <i class=b>range</i>(<i class=n>100</i>)]\n        results = [future.result() <i class=k>for</i> future <i class=k>in</i> concurrent.futures.as_completed(futures)]\n    \n    <i class=b>print</i>(<i class=s>f&quot;Concurrent test stats: <i class=u>{pool.get_stats()}</i>&quot;</i>)\n    <i Pressure</strong>: Fewer objects for garbage collection</li>\n<li><strong>Predictable Performance</strong>: Avoid allocation spikes</li>\n<li><strong>Memory Locality</strong>: Objects stay in cache particle.vy = vy\n            particle.life = life\n            particle.active = <i class=l>True</i>\n            <i class=v>self</i>.active_indices.append(index)\n    \n    <i class=k>def</i> <i particle.vx\n                particle.y += particle.vy\n                particle.life -= <i class=n>1</i>\n                \n                <i class=k>if</i> particle.life &gt; <i class=n>0</i>:\n                    <i class=v>self</i>.active_indices.append(index)\n                <i class=k>else</i>:\n                    particle.active = <i class=l>False</i>\n                    <i <i class=b>dict</i>:\n        <i class=k>return</i> {\n            <i class=s>&#x27;objects&#x27;</i>: <i class=b>len</i>(gc.get_objects()),\n            <i class=s>&#x27;garbage&#x27;</i>: <i class=b>len</i>(gc.garbage),\n            <i class=s>&#x27;collections&#x27;</i>: gc.get_count()\n        }\n    \n<i class=m>    @staticmethod</i>\n    <i class=k>def</i> <i class=f>measure_allocation</i>(<i class=p>name: <i class=b>str</i>, func</i>):\n        <i class=c># Disable automatic GC for accurate measurement</i>\n        gc.disable()\n        \n        before_stats = GCMonitor.get_memory_usage()\n        before_time = time.time()\n        \n        <i class=k>try</i>:\n            result = func()\n        <i class=k>finally</i>:\n            after_time = time.time()\n            after_stats = GCMonitor.get_memory_usage()\n            \n            gc.enable()\n            gc.collect()  <i class=c># Force cleanup</i>\n            \n            <i class=b>print</i>(<i class=s>f&quot;\n<i class=u>{name}</i>:&quot;</i>)\n            <i class=b>print</i>(<i class=s>f&quot; Languages:</strong></p>\n<ol>\n<li><strong>Minimize Object Creation</strong>: Use object pools, flyweight pattern</li>\n<li><strong>Avoid Reference Cycles</strong>: Use weak references where appropriate</li>\n<li><strong>Design for Generations</strong>: Young objects die quickly, old objects persist</li>\n<li><strong>Use Value Types</strong>: Prefer primitives and structs over objects when possible</li>\n<li><strong>Batch Operations</strong>: Group allocations to reduce GC pressure</li>\n<li><strong>Monitor Memory Usage</strong>: Profile allocation patterns and GC behavior</li>\n<li><strong>Lazy Initialization</strong>: Create objects only when needed</li>\n<li><strong>Immutable Objects</strong>: Can be optimized by GC and enable Advantages:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li>Single memory allocation for object hierarchy</li>\n<li>Direct method calls through vtable (faster dispatch)</li>\n<li>Shared code across inheritance chain reduces memory footprint</li>\n<li>Compiler optimizations like inlining work better</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>Inheritance Disadvantages:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li>Deep inheritance chains increase method lookup time</li>\n<li>Larger object sizes due to parent class data</li>\n<li>Cache misses from scattered memory layout</li>\n<li>Less flexible for runtime behavior changes</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>Composition Advantages:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li>Better cache locality for individual components</li>\n<li>Flexible object assembly at runtime</li>\n<li>Smaller memory footprint per component</li>\n<li>Easy to optimize individual components</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>Composition Disadvantages:</strong></p>\n<ul>\n<li>Additional indirection through object references</li>\n<li>More memory allocations</li>\n<li>Method calls through delegation (extra overhead)</li>\n</ul>\n<p><strong>JavaScript Example:</strong></p>\n<div class=\"code\"><div
